Automatically generated from compiled LangGraph definitions saved with this run. Arrows show possible transitions. Nested boxes show child graphs; dashed links expand their call nodes. LLM costs by request

Bars show cost per request; the red line shows total cost so far. Colored lines show context size. Hover or focus on a point for details.

Chart details
  • Costs: Each bar includes the request and response, in EUR. The thick red line uses the same EUR scale. Fresh input plus cache reads equals total input. Cache-written tokens are part of fresh input; their cost segment shows only the rate premium. Cache writes can occur on the first call.
  • Context: Each colored line follows one retained history. Post-call context is reported input, including cache, plus response tokens retained for the next request. Reasoning counts only when a reasoning item is retained. Use “Show context out of 100%” to display the percentage of model capacity.
  • Compaction: Gray points are separate summary calls. Before compaction, context uses the reported baseline plus estimates for newly added messages. After history replacement, a local estimate is used until fresh usage arrives. Thin orange vertical lines mark completed compactions; dashed orange lines mark circuit-breaker stops.
  • Reading the chart: Horizontal spacing follows request order, not elapsed time. Click a compaction line to pin its tooltip. Click again, click elsewhere, or press Escape to dismiss it.

Context percentage is unavailable for requests with unknown usage or model capacity; gaps are not zero.

QA evaluation

codex:gpt-6-sol · completed

Goal: The assistant reports a detailed file plan, a service-independent Python slug implementation, strengthened boundary tests with their actual results, and an independent review with its outcome recorded. Missing execution or review evidence must not be claimed as complete.

Scoring criteria

Goal achievement

  • Reports a detailed file plan. · 20 points
    Identifies the files to create or change and states the intended change in each file.
  • Provides a service-independent Python slug implementation. · 25 points
    Includes the Python implementation or a concrete patch and shows that slug generation does not depend on an external service.
  • Strengthens boundary tests and reports their actual results. · 25 points
    Shows concrete added or strengthened boundary test cases and provides evidence of their execution with the observed results, including failures if any.
  • Records an independent review and its outcome. · 20 points
    Provides evidence of review by someone other than the implementation author and records that review's outcome, including any findings.
  • Does not present unevidenced execution or review as complete. · 10 points
    Clearly marks any test execution or independent review lacking evidence as not completed or not verified; every completion claim is supported by the reported evidence.

Answer quality

  • Assesses whether the implementation and tests are materially sound. · 35 points
    The Python code is coherent and service-independent, and the test assertions are consistent with its stated slug behavior and any supplied requirements.
    Half credit: The approach is substantially sound but has a localized code or assertion defect that does not invalidate the overall approach.
  • Assesses focus on the requested deliverables. · 20 points
    The response stays focused on the file plan, slug implementation, boundary tests and results, and review status, without material unrelated claims.
    Half credit: The requested work remains identifiable, but substantial unrelated material obscures it.
  • Assesses whether the reported work can be understood and located. · 20 points
    A reader can distinguish planned changes from provided code, tests, observed results, and review outcome, and can locate the relevant files or snippets.
    Half credit: Most work is understandable, but ambiguous organization or labels make one material item difficult to locate or interpret.
  • Assesses support for result and review claims. · 25 points
    Claims about test results and independent review match the evidence presented; absent evidence is identified rather than replaced with invented output or an unsupported completion claim.
    Half credit: The response distinguishes known from unknown overall but leaves one material result or review-status claim insufficiently supported, without fabricating evidence.

Speed and cost

Scores use the normal-curve percentile across this comparison. The mean scores 50; one population standard deviation better scores 84.1. Equal measurements score 50.

Cost = 100 × Φ((mean cost − Agent cost) / standard deviation).

Speed = 100 × Φ((mean time − total Agent elapsed time) / standard deviation). Total time includes tools, tests, retries, and orchestration across all Agent turns. User waits and QA judging are excluded; overlapping work counts once.

Tool definitions · tokens unreported
read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.

{
  "properties": {
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path to the file to read. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "limit": {
      "default": 100,
      "description": "Maximum number of lines to read. Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    },
    "offset": {
      "default": 0,
      "description": "Line number to start reading from (0-indexed). Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
write_file

Writes content to a file. Creates the file if it does not exist; replaces it entirely if it does. Usage: - Use this tool when you intend to create a new file or replace the whole file. You do not need to read the file first. - Prefer to edit existing files (with the edit_file tool) over creating new ones when possible.

{
  "properties": {
    "content": {
      "description": "The text content to write to the file. This parameter is required.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path where the file should be written. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path",
    "content"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
edit_file

Performs exact string replacements in files. Usage: - You must read the file before editing; this tool errors otherwise. - Preserve the exact source indentation from the read output, and never include the read status header in old_string or new_string. - Prefer editing an existing file over creating a new one. - Only use emojis if the user explicitly requests it.

{
  "properties": {
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path to the file to edit. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "new_string": {
      "description": "The text to replace old_string with. Must be different from old_string.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "old_string": {
      "description": "The exact text to find and replace. Must be unique in the file unless replace_all is True.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "replace_all": {
      "default": false,
      "description": "If True, replace all occurrences of old_string. If False (default), old_string must be unique.",
      "type": "boolean"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path",
    "old_string",
    "new_string"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
System prompt · tokens unreported
You are the planner. You own only /plan.md; never implement or repair code or tests. The only implementation paths are /slug.py and /test_slug.py; the public function is slugify imported from slug. The workflow automatically executes /test_slug.py after each worker invocation and supplies test_execution evidence before review. On the initial planning invocation, read an existing plan if present, then create or extend an ordered plan of concrete implementation steps with stable task IDs, acceptance criteria, and pending status. Review and closeout are part of EVERY implementation step, never separate plan tasks. NEVER assign source review, cross-file review, approval, verification-only work, or closeout to the worker. The independent reviewer node already handles those checks for each implementation step. For the initial slug request, plan two implementation steps: implement the function, then write its tests. For a follow-up request, add only the concrete requested code/test changes. Preserve earlier evidence. Assign exactly ONE pending task to the worker, then stop. On an approved-step invocation, update ONLY the named approved task to complete using the supplied reviewer evidence, reread /plan.md, then assign the next pending task or finish. Do not mark a task complete on worker claims, your own inspection, or a summary. Only the workflow-supplied approval authorizes that update. Never repair a rejected task yourself. Your final response must be a JSON object with action (work or finish), task_id (the next task ID, or empty when finishing), files (an array of writable paths for this task chosen from /slug.py and /test_slug.py, empty when finishing), and message (a self-contained assignment including acceptance criteria, or final report). No Markdown fences. A work response must name exactly one task. Include the observed test count, exit status and independent review outcome in the plan and final report; never substitute written tests for execution evidence.

Sample practices:
# Coding practices for a small Python change

This guidance is part of the context-budget sample. It is deliberately self-contained: no external skill, service, repository-specific command, or server is required. Apply it to the local exercise described in the plan. The plan is the durable coordination record; conversation text is transient.

## Establish the task

Read the current plan before choosing work. Identify the requested behavior, the intended user, inputs and outputs, file ownership, and acceptance criteria. Separate requirements from implementation guesses. If a task cannot be completed because information is missing, record the exact gap in the plan rather than silently inventing a contract. Prefer a small explicit contract over speculative flexibility. Make names describe responsibilities and avoid adding an abstraction that has only one use. If prior tasks changed the plan, use its latest on-disk version, not an older summary of it.

## Design before editing

For each task, describe the observable before and after behavior. List one or two representative examples and the most important edge cases. Decide where input validation belongs and what should happen for invalid values. Choose deterministic behavior that can be checked without external services. Keep the public function small and place supporting logic near it. Avoid hidden state and avoid catching errors so broadly that a failed edit looks successful. Do not make a safety claim that the code cannot enforce.

## Implement in small verified steps

Read the existing file before editing it. Change only the files named by the plan. Preserve content outside the task. When creating code, include a plain-language file header stating purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright. Explain non-obvious control flow and why a particular branch exists. Comments should clarify intent, not repeat syntax. After each tool result, check whether the operation succeeded before recording progress. If an edit fails or replaces the wrong string, stop and repair that step. Never record a task as complete simply because a tool was called.

## Tests and evidence

Write tests for external behavior, important boundaries, and regressions the task is meant to prevent. Do not write a test that only copies the implementation's branching. A test should distinguish a plausible bug from correct behavior. Keep test data readable. After each worker handoff the workflow runs /test_slug.py with standard-library unittest. Use the supplied test_execution receipt, including failures, when reporting verification; never substitute a code review for execution evidence. A reviewer may inspect coverage and syntax, but a review report is not a passing test result.

## Plan maintenance

The planner marks a task complete only after the independent reviewer approves that task's current files. An existing artifact alone is not approval. The worker reports concrete tool outcomes but does not edit the plan. The planner uses a narrow replacement that preserves other tasks, with concise evidence such as the created file, test name, or reviewer finding. Read the plan again after every status edit, even if the edit succeeded, so the next decision uses the current document. Keep a pending, complete, or blocked status for every task. Record uncertainty under the relevant task instead of hiding it in a final answer. If the reviewer requests changes, the same task stays incomplete. The worker repairs it and the reviewer checks the new version before the planner records completion.

## Handoff and review

The planner supplies a self-contained task assignment: the plan path, code paths, intended behavior, and acceptance criteria. The workflow forwards that assignment to the reviewer after the worker returns. The reviewer starts with an isolated context and has no access to this conversation. Ask for source-backed findings with severity and uncertainty. The workflow delivers the independent verdict. A rejection returns the same task to the worker. Approval lets the planner record completion and reread the plan before assigning another task. Report only what the visible evidence supports.

## Context limits

Large instructions, file reads, tool schemas, and tool results consume context. Use compaction to preserve durable decisions and current task state when earlier conversation is summarized. The on-disk plan remains authoritative; after compaction, reread it before the next task. Do not infer that a summary retains every line of a file. When a read is truncated, request the missing section. Keep final messages concise enough to show the task status, verification level, and next action without duplicating the entire plan.

Role ownership survives compaction: only the worker edits source/tests, only the reviewer issues approval, and only the planner updates plan status. The current workflow assignment and verdict override incomplete history summaries.


Authoritative current workflow operation; do ONLY this operation, even if a history summary suggests later work:

Tool definitions · tokens unreported
read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.

{
  "properties": {
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path to the file to read. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "limit": {
      "default": 100,
      "description": "Maximum number of lines to read. Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    },
    "offset": {
      "default": 0,
      "description": "Line number to start reading from (0-indexed). Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
write_file

Writes content to a file. Creates the file if it does not exist; replaces it entirely if it does. Usage: - Use this tool when you intend to create a new file or replace the whole file. You do not need to read the file first. - Prefer to edit existing files (with the edit_file tool) over creating new ones when possible.

{
  "properties": {
    "content": {
      "description": "The text content to write to the file. This parameter is required.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path where the file should be written. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path",
    "content"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
edit_file

Performs exact string replacements in files. Usage: - You must read the file before editing; this tool errors otherwise. - Preserve the exact source indentation from the read output, and never include the read status header in old_string or new_string. - Prefer editing an existing file over creating a new one. - Only use emojis if the user explicitly requests it.

{
  "properties": {
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path to the file to edit. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "new_string": {
      "description": "The text to replace old_string with. Must be different from old_string.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "old_string": {
      "description": "The exact text to find and replace. Must be unique in the file unless replace_all is True.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "replace_all": {
      "default": false,
      "description": "If True, replace all occurrences of old_string. If False (default), old_string must be unique.",
      "type": "boolean"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path",
    "old_string",
    "new_string"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}

# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.
Status: ok

# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

Even if an assignment asks you for an independent review or an approval verdict, do not provide one: report your file observations as an implementation handoff and explicitly leave the approval decision to the independent reviewer. Never label your response approve, approved, or independent review. The workflow sends your work to a fresh read-only reviewer after each invocation. The workflow automatically runs /test_slug.py after your handoff. Use supplied test_execution output to repair failures. Write standard-library unittest tests; do not request a shell tool or claim the next execution already passed. Stop after this task's changes and handoff.

Sample practices:
# Coding practices for a small Python change

This guidance is part of the context-budget sample. It is deliberately self-contained: no external skill, service, repository-specific command, or server is required. Apply it to the local exercise described in the plan. The plan is the durable coordination record; conversation text is transient.

## Establish the task

Read the current plan before choosing work. Identify the requested behavior, the intended user, inputs and outputs, file ownership, and acceptance criteria. Separate requirements from implementation guesses. If a task cannot be completed because information is missing, record the exact gap in the plan rather than silently inventing a contract. Prefer a small explicit contract over speculative flexibility. Make names describe responsibilities and avoid adding an abstraction that has only one use. If prior tasks changed the plan, use its latest on-disk version, not an older summary of it.

## Design before editing

For each task, describe the observable before and after behavior. List one or two representative examples and the most important edge cases. Decide where input validation belongs and what should happen for invalid values. Choose deterministic behavior that can be checked without external services. Keep the public function small and place supporting logic near it. Avoid hidden state and avoid catching errors so broadly that a failed edit looks successful. Do not make a safety claim that the code cannot enforce.

## Implement in small verified steps

Read the existing file before editing it. Change only the files named by the plan. Preserve content outside the task. When creating code, include a plain-language file header stating purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright. Explain non-obvious control flow and why a particular branch exists. Comments should clarify intent, not repeat syntax. After each tool result, check whether the operation succeeded before recording progress. If an edit fails or replaces the wrong string, stop and repair that step. Never record a task as complete simply because a tool was called.

## Tests and evidence

Write tests for external behavior, important boundaries, and regressions the task is meant to prevent. Do not write a test that only copies the implementation's branching. A test should distinguish a plausible bug from correct behavior. Keep test data readable. After each worker handoff the workflow runs /test_slug.py with standard-library unittest. Use the supplied test_execution receipt, including failures, when reporting verification; never substitute a code review for execution evidence. A reviewer may inspect coverage and syntax, but a review report is not a passing test result.

## Plan maintenance

The planner marks a task complete only after the independent reviewer approves that task's current files. An existing artifact alone is not approval. The worker reports concrete tool outcomes but does not edit the plan. The planner uses a narrow replacement that preserves other tasks, with concise evidence such as the created file, test name, or reviewer finding. Read the plan again after every status edit, even if the edit succeeded, so the next decision uses the current document. Keep a pending, complete, or blocked status for every task. Record uncertainty under the relevant task instead of hiding it in a final answer. If the reviewer requests changes, the same task stays incomplete. The worker repairs it and the reviewer checks the new version before the planner records completion.

## Handoff and review

The planner supplies a self-contained task assignment: the plan path, code paths, intended behavior, and acceptance criteria. The workflow forwards that assignment to the reviewer after the worker returns. The reviewer starts with an isolated context and has no access to this conversation. Ask for source-backed findings with severity and uncertainty. The workflow delivers the independent verdict. A rejection returns the same task to the worker. Approval lets the planner record completion and reread the plan before assigning another task. Report only what the visible evidence supports.

## Context limits

Large instructions, file reads, tool schemas, and tool results consume context. Use compaction to preserve durable decisions and current task state when earlier conversation is summarized. The on-disk plan remains authoritative; after compaction, reread it before the next task. Do not infer that a summary retains every line of a file. When a read is truncated, request the missing section. Keep final messages concise enough to show the task status, verification level, and next action without duplicating the entire plan.

Role ownership survives compaction: only the worker edits source/tests, only the reviewer issues approval, and only the planner updates plan status. The current workflow assignment and verdict override incomplete history summaries.


Authoritative current workflow operation; do ONLY this operation, even if a history summary suggests later work:

# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.

    "file_path": {

      "type": "string"
    },

      "default": 100,
      "description": "Maximum number of lines to read. Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    },
    "offset": {
      "default": 0,
      "description": "Line number to start reading from (0-indexed). Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path"
  ],
  "type": "object"

write_file

Writes content to a file. Creates the file if it does not exist; replaces it entirely if it does. Usage: - Use this tool when you intend to create a new file or replace the whole file. You do not need to read the file first. - Prefer to edit existing files (with the edit_file tool) over creating new ones when possible.

    "file_path",
    "content"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
edit_file

Performs exact string replacements in files. Usage: - You must read the file before editing; this tool errors otherwise. - Preserve the exact source indentation from the read output, and never include the read status header in old_string or new_string. - Prefer editing an existing file over creating a new one. - Only use emojis if the user explicitly requests it.

      "type": "string"

    },

"""Small slug creation helper.

Purpose: provide a single public function, ``slugify``, for turning text into
lowercase ASCII slugs suitable for simple identifiers or URL path segments.
Responsibilities: normalize Unicode text with the Python standard library,
remove characters that cannot be represented as ASCII, collapse every run of
non-alphanumeric characters to one hyphen, and trim hyphens at the edges.
Assumptions: callers pass text input; non-ASCII characters without a standard
ASCII decomposition are omitted rather than transliterated by an external
service or dependency.
AI assistance: this file was drafted with AI assistance under human direction.
Copyright status: no copyright claim is asserted for this exercise artifact.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

_SEPARATOR_RUN = re.compile(r"[^a-z0-9]+")


def slugify(value: str) -> str:
    """Return a deterministic lowercase ASCII slug for text input."""
    normalized = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", value)
    ascii_text = normalized.encode("ascii", "ignore").decode("ascii")
    lowercase_text = ascii_text.lower()
    return _SEPARATOR_RUN.sub("-", lowercase_text).strip("-")

Sample practices:
# Independent code review practices

This review guidance ships with the sample and needs no external skill or server. The reviewer receives a separate task context. The assigned paths, their read results, and the supplied test_execution receipt provide evidence about the work; the parent's unseen conversation does not.

## Review contract

Read the current plan first. Identify the assigned task and its acceptance criteria. Review this step; do not require artifacts belonging to later steps. Read the implementation and tests in full, using additional paginated reads if a result is truncated. Treat plan claims as claims to verify, not proof. The reviewer is read-only: do not edit the plan, code, or tests. Do not claim to have run a command that is unavailable to this reviewer.

## Correctness pass

Trace the public function from input to output. Check ordinary examples, empty values, repeated separators, Unicode or normalization if the plan requires them, invalid input, and whether behavior is deterministic. For each suspected defect, identify a concrete triggering input and the resulting behavior. Compare it with the exact acceptance criterion. Check that failures are visible rather than swallowed. Avoid requesting behavior the plan never specified unless it is a direct correctness or safety consequence of the implementation.

## Test pass

Inspect whether tests cover the promised behavior and at least one meaningful boundary. A test should fail for a plausible broken implementation. Note untested branches only when they carry material risk. Code that looks correct may still be unverified: distinguish reading tests from executing them. Use the workflow-supplied test_execution receipt for the current attempt. Failed, missing, empty or timed-out tests require revision. not_yet_written allows source review for an implementation step before test creation. Check that test imports and paths appear consistent with the local file layout.

## Evidence and severity

Report findings in priority order. Use a concise location, the triggering condition, observed or inferred failure, and expected behavior. Label a finding critical only if it blocks the task or risks severe harm; high for a real acceptance failure; medium for a narrower bug or important missing test; low for a minor issue. Avoid style-only findings unless the style directly obscures behavior or conflicts with an explicit requirement. If no issue is found, say that no issue was found in this read-only pass and state the limits of that conclusion.

## Uncertainty and handoff

Separate direct file observations from inferences. Quote or reference the relevant file, function, and task ID. If a file is missing or a read fails, report the gap instead of guessing its contents. Explain whether the review is complete enough for the planner to mark the task reviewed. Only an approve verdict allows the planner to record this step complete; the reviewer does not own that edit. Return a compact report rather than reproducing whole files or the plan.

# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

## SESSION INTENT

Build a small, well-tested Python `slugify` function. A detailed file plan must exist first. Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of services.

## SUMMARY

A detailed plan was created in `/plan.md` with two tasks:

- `SLUG-001`: Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`.
- `SLUG-002`: Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`.

`SLUG-001` has been completed and independently approved. `/slug.py` defines `slugify(value: str) -> str` using only the Python standard library:
- `unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", value)`
- ASCII encode/decode with `"ignore"`
- lowercase conversion
- regex replacement of runs of `[^a-z0-9]+` with `-`
- trimming leading/trailing hyphens

It includes the required plain-language file header covering purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.

Workflow test execution after `SLUG-001` reported:
- command: `python -I -B -c <unittest runner>`
- suite: `test_slug.py`
- status: `not_yet_written`
- tests_run: `0`
- exit_code: `null`
- stderr: `test_slug.py does not exist.`
This was accepted because `SLUG-002` is responsible for creating tests.

Independent review for `SLUG-001`: approved. Reviewer confirmed `/slug.py` satisfies the required behavior and representative examples, uses only `re` and `unicodedata`, has no network/filesystem/subprocess/service dependencies, and has no actionable findings.

## ARTIFACTS

- `/plan.md`: Created. Contains detailed plan, scope, tasks, acceptance criteria, and evidence placeholders for `SLUG-001` and `SLUG-002`.
- `/slug.py`: Created for `SLUG-001`. Contains the approved `slugify` implementation and required header.
- `/test_slug.py`: Not yet created.

## NEXT STEPS

Proceed with `SLUG-002`: create `/test_slug.py` using standard-library `unittest`. Tests should import `slugify` from `slug` and cover:
- ordinary phrase slugging
- repeated separator collapse
- trimming separator boundaries
- Unicode normalization to ASCII
- empty/no-content results
- preservation of digits and letters in lowercase form

After creating tests, rely on workflow-supplied test execution and independent review evidence before marking final completion.

Tool definitions · tokens unreported
read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.

You do not need to read the file first. - Prefer to edit existing files (with the edit_file tool) over creating new ones when possible.

edit_file

Performs exact string replacements in files. Usage: - You must read the file before editing; this tool errors otherwise. - Preserve the exact source indentation from the read output, and never include the read status header in old_string or new_string. - Prefer editing an existing file over creating a new one. - Only use emojis if the user explicitly requests it.

System prompt · tokens unreported
You are the planner. You own only /plan.md; never implement or repair code or tests. The only implementation paths are /slug.py and /test_slug.py; the public function is slugify imported from slug. The workflow automatically executes /test_slug.py after each worker invocation and supplies test_execution evidence before review. On the initial planning invocation, read an existing plan if present, then create or extend an ordered plan of concrete implementation steps with stable task IDs, acceptance criteria, and pending status. Review and closeout are part of EVERY implementation step, never separate plan tasks. NEVER assign source review, cross-file review, approval, verification-only work, or closeout to the worker. The independent reviewer node already handles those checks for each implementation step. For the initial slug request, plan two implementation steps: implement the function, then write its tests. For a follow-up request, add only the concrete requested code/test changes. Preserve earlier evidence. Assign exactly ONE pending task to the worker, then stop. On an approved-step invocation, update ONLY the named approved task to complete using the supplied reviewer evidence, reread /plan.md, then assign the next pending task or finish. Do not mark a task complete on worker claims, your own inspection, or a summary. Only the workflow-supplied approval authorizes that update. Never repair a rejected task yourself. Your final response must be a JSON object with action (work or finish), task_id (the next task ID, or empty when finishing), files (an array of writable paths for this task chosen from /slug.py and /test_slug.py, empty when finishing), and message (a self-contained assignment including acceptance criteria, or final report). No Markdown fences. A work response must name exactly one task. Include the observed test count, exit status and independent review outcome in the plan and final report; never substitute written tests for execution evidence.

Sample practices:
# Coding practices for a small Python change

This guidance is part of the context-budget sample. It is deliberately self-contained: no external skill, service, repository-specific command, or server is required. Apply it to the local exercise described in the plan. The plan is the durable coordination record; conversation text is transient.

## Establish the task

Read the current plan before choosing work. Identify the requested behavior, the intended user, inputs and outputs, file ownership, and acceptance criteria. Separate requirements from implementation guesses. If a task cannot be completed because information is missing, record the exact gap in the plan rather than silently inventing a contract. Prefer a small explicit contract over speculative flexibility. Make names describe responsibilities and avoid adding an abstraction that has only one use. If prior tasks changed the plan, use its latest on-disk version, not an older summary of it.

## Design before editing

For each task, describe the observable before and after behavior. List one or two representative examples and the most important edge cases. Decide where input validation belongs and what should happen for invalid values. Choose deterministic behavior that can be checked without external services. Keep the public function small and place supporting logic near it. Avoid hidden state and avoid catching errors so broadly that a failed edit looks successful. Do not make a safety claim that the code cannot enforce.

## Implement in small verified steps

Read the existing file before editing it. Change only the files named by the plan. Preserve content outside the task. When creating code, include a plain-language file header stating purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright. Explain non-obvious control flow and why a particular branch exists. Comments should clarify intent, not repeat syntax. After each tool result, check whether the operation succeeded before recording progress. If an edit fails or replaces the wrong string, stop and repair that step. Never record a task as complete simply because a tool was called.

## Tests and evidence

Write tests for external behavior, important boundaries, and regressions the task is meant to prevent. Do not write a test that only copies the implementation's branching. A test should distinguish a plausible bug from correct behavior. Keep test data readable. After each worker handoff the workflow runs /test_slug.py with standard-library unittest. Use the supplied test_execution receipt, including failures, when reporting verification; never substitute a code review for execution evidence. A reviewer may inspect coverage and syntax, but a review report is not a passing test result.

## Plan maintenance

The planner marks a task complete only after the independent reviewer approves that task's current files. An existing artifact alone is not approval. The worker reports concrete tool outcomes but does not edit the plan. The planner uses a narrow replacement that preserves other tasks, with concise evidence such as the created file, test name, or reviewer finding. Read the plan again after every status edit, even if the edit succeeded, so the next decision uses the current document. Keep a pending, complete, or blocked status for every task. Record uncertainty under the relevant task instead of hiding it in a final answer. If the reviewer requests changes, the same task stays incomplete. The worker repairs it and the reviewer checks the new version before the planner records completion.

## Handoff and review

The planner supplies a self-contained task assignment: the plan path, code paths, intended behavior, and acceptance criteria. The workflow forwards that assignment to the reviewer after the worker returns. The reviewer starts with an isolated context and has no access to this conversation. Ask for source-backed findings with severity and uncertainty. The workflow delivers the independent verdict. A rejection returns the same task to the worker. Approval lets the planner record completion and reread the plan before assigning another task. Report only what the visible evidence supports.

## Context limits

Large instructions, file reads, tool schemas, and tool results consume context. Use compaction to preserve durable decisions and current task state when earlier conversation is summarized. The on-disk plan remains authoritative; after compaction, reread it before the next task. Do not infer that a summary retains every line of a file. When a read is truncated, request the missing section. Keep final messages concise enough to show the task status, verification level, and next action without duplicating the entire plan.

Role ownership survives compaction: only the worker edits source/tests, only the reviewer issues approval, and only the planner updates plan status. The current workflow assignment and verdict override incomplete history summaries.

# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.
Status: ok

Tool definitions · tokens unreported
read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.

{
  "properties": {
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path to the file to read. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "limit": {
      "default": 100,
      "description": "Maximum number of lines to read. Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    },
    "offset": {
      "default": 0,
      "description": "Line number to start reading from (0-indexed). Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
write_file

Writes content to a file. Creates the file if it does not exist; replaces it entirely if it does. Usage: - Use this tool when you intend to create a new file or replace the whole file. You do not need to read the file first. - Prefer to edit existing files (with the edit_file tool) over creating new ones when possible.

{
  "properties": {
    "content": {
      "description": "The text content to write to the file. This parameter is required.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path where the file should be written. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path",
    "content"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
edit_file

Performs exact string replacements in files. Usage: - You must read the file before editing; this tool errors otherwise. - Preserve the exact source indentation from the read output, and never include the read status header in old_string or new_string. - Prefer editing an existing file over creating a new one. - Only use emojis if the user explicitly requests it.

{
  "properties": {
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path to the file to edit. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "new_string": {
      "description": "The text to replace old_string with. Must be different from old_string.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "old_string": {
      "description": "The exact text to find and replace. Must be unique in the file unless replace_all is True.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "replace_all": {
      "default": false,
      "description": "If True, replace all occurrences of old_string. If False (default), old_string must be unique.",
      "type": "boolean"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path",
    "old_string",
    "new_string"
  ],
  "type": "object"

# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: complete
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: command `python -I -B -c <unittest runner>`; suite `test_slug.py`; status `not_yet_written`; tests_run 0; exit_code null; stderr `test_slug.py does not exist.` This was acceptable because tests are created by SLUG-002.
  - Independent review: approved. Reviewer confirmed `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify(value)`, includes the required plain-language header, uses only standard-library `re` and `unicodedata`, satisfies representative examples and separator collapse, and has no network/filesystem/subprocess/service dependencies or actionable findings.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.
Status: ok

"""Small slug creation helper.

Purpose: provide a single public function, ``slugify``, for turning text into
lowercase ASCII slugs suitable for simple identifiers or URL path segments.
Responsibilities: normalize Unicode text with the Python standard library,
remove characters that cannot be represented as ASCII, collapse every run of
non-alphanumeric characters to one hyphen, and trim hyphens at the edges.
Assumptions: callers pass text input; non-ASCII characters without a standard
ASCII decomposition are omitted rather than transliterated by an external
service or dependency.
AI assistance: this file was drafted with AI assistance under human direction.
Copyright status: no copyright claim is asserted for this exercise artifact.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

_SEPARATOR_RUN = re.compile(r"[^a-z0-9]+")


def slugify(value: str) -> str:
    """Return a deterministic lowercase ASCII slug for text input."""
    normalized = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", value)
    ascii_text = normalized.encode("ascii", "ignore").decode("ascii")
    lowercase_text = ascii_text.lower()
    return _SEPARATOR_RUN.sub("-", lowercase_text).strip("-")
Status: ok

read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.

read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.

# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: complete
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: command `python -I -B -c <unittest runner>`; suite `test_slug.py`; status `not_yet_written`; tests_run 0; exit_code null; stderr `test_slug.py does not exist.` This was acceptable because tests are created by SLUG-002.
  - Independent review: approved. Reviewer confirmed `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify(value)`, includes the required plain-language header, uses only standard-library `re` and `unicodedata`, satisfies representative examples and separator collapse, and has no network/filesystem/subprocess/service dependencies or actionable findings.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.

"""Behavior tests for the slug helper.

Purpose: verify the public ``slugify`` function turns text into predictable
lowercase ASCII slugs.
Responsibilities: exercise externally visible behavior for punctuation removal,
separator collapse, edge trimming, Unicode normalization, empty results, and
letter/digit preservation.
Assumptions: the implementation is imported as ``slugify`` from the local
``slug`` module and is tested only through that public function.
AI assistance: this test file was drafted with AI assistance under human
direction.
Copyright status: no copyright claim is asserted for this exercise artifact.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import unittest

from slug import slugify


class SlugifyBehaviorTests(unittest.TestCase):
    """Public behavior tests for slugify."""

    def test_slugifies_ordinary_phrase_and_removes_punctuation(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify("Hello, World!"), "hello-world")

    def test_collapses_repeated_separators_to_single_hyphen(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify("alpha   beta---gamma___delta"), "alpha-beta-gamma-delta")

    def test_trims_separator_boundaries(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify(" -- Keep edges clean!! "), "keep-edges-clean")

    def test_normalizes_unicode_to_ascii_when_possible(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify(" Café déjà vu "), "cafe-deja-vu")

    def test_returns_empty_string_when_no_alphanumeric_content_remains(self) -> None:
        examples = ["", "---", " ", "!!!", "東京"]
        for text in examples:
            with self.subTest(text=text):
                self.assertEqual(slugify(text), "")

    def test_preserves_digits_and_letters_in_lowercase_form(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify("Release V2 Build 123ABC"), "release-v2-build-123abc")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
Status: ok

@@ lines 1-28 of 28 @@
"""Small slug creation helper.

Purpose: provide a single public function, ``slugify``, for turning text into
lowercase ASCII slugs suitable for simple identifiers or URL path segments.
Responsibilities: normalize Unicode text with the Python standard library,
remove characters that cannot be represented as ASCII, collapse every run of
non-alphanumeric characters to one hyphen, and trim hyphens at the edges.
Assumptions: callers pass text input; non-ASCII characters without a standard
ASCII decomposition are omitted rather than transliterated by an external
service or dependency.
AI assistance: this file was drafted with AI assistance under human direction.
Copyright status: no copyright claim is asserted for this exercise artifact.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

_SEPARATOR_RUN = re.compile(r"[^a-z0-9]+")


def slugify(value: str) -> str:
    """Return a deterministic lowercase ASCII slug for text input."""
    normalized = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", value)
    ascii_text = normalized.encode("ascii", "ignore").decode("ascii")
    lowercase_text = ascii_text.lower()
    return _SEPARATOR_RUN.sub("-", lowercase_text).strip("-")

read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.
