# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: complete
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: command `python -I -B -c <unittest runner>`; suite `test_slug.py`; status `not_yet_written`; tests_run 0; exit_code null; stderr `test_slug.py does not exist.` This was acceptable because tests are created by SLUG-002.
  - Independent review: approved. Reviewer confirmed `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify(value)`, includes the required plain-language header, uses only standard-library `re` and `unicodedata`, satisfies representative examples and separator collapse, and has no network/filesystem/subprocess/service dependencies or actionable findings.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

"""Small slug creation helper.

Purpose: provide a single public function, ``slugify``, for turning text into
lowercase ASCII slugs suitable for simple identifiers or URL path segments.
Responsibilities: normalize Unicode text with the Python standard library,
remove characters that cannot be represented as ASCII, collapse every run of
non-alphanumeric characters to one hyphen, and trim hyphens at the edges.
Assumptions: callers pass text input; non-ASCII characters without a standard
ASCII decomposition are omitted rather than transliterated by an external
service or dependency.
AI assistance: this file was drafted with AI assistance under human direction.
Copyright status: no copyright claim is asserted for this exercise artifact.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

_SEPARATOR_RUN = re.compile(r"[^a-z0-9]+")


def slugify(value: str) -> str:
    """Return a deterministic lowercase ASCII slug for text input."""
    normalized = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", value)
    ascii_text = normalized.encode("ascii", "ignore").decode("ascii")
    lowercase_text = ascii_text.lower()
    return _SEPARATOR_RUN.sub("-", lowercase_text).strip("-")

read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.

# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: complete
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: command `python -I -B -c <unittest runner>`; suite `test_slug.py`; status `not_yet_written`; tests_run 0; exit_code null; stderr `test_slug.py does not exist.` This was acceptable because tests are created by SLUG-002.
  - Independent review: approved. Reviewer confirmed `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify(value)`, includes the required plain-language header, uses only standard-library `re` and `unicodedata`, satisfies representative examples and separator collapse, and has no network/filesystem/subprocess/service dependencies or actionable findings.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: complete
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: command `python -I -B -c <unittest runner>`; suite `test_slug.py`; status `passed`; tests_run 6; exit_code 0; failures 0; errors 0; skipped 0; stderr reported all six tests ok and `Ran 6 tests in 0.001s` / `OK`.
  - Independent review: approved. Reviewer confirmed `/test_slug.py` has the required plain-language header, imports `slugify` from `slug`, uses standard-library `unittest` with a `unittest.main()` entry point, defines six public-behavior tests covering required cases, distinguishes plausible incorrect implementations, and did not show evidence that `/slug.py` was modified for this task.
Status: ok

Tool definitions · tokens unreported
read_file

Reads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.

{
  "properties": {
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path to the file to read. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "limit": {
      "default": 100,
      "description": "Maximum number of lines to read. Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    },
    "offset": {
      "default": 0,
      "description": "Line number to start reading from (0-indexed). Use for pagination of large files.",
      "type": "integer"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
write_file

Writes content to a file. Creates the file if it does not exist; replaces it entirely if it does. Usage: - Use this tool when you intend to create a new file or replace the whole file. You do not need to read the file first. - Prefer to edit existing files (with the edit_file tool) over creating new ones when possible.

{
  "properties": {
    "content": {
      "description": "The text content to write to the file. This parameter is required.",

    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path where the file should be written. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path",
    "content"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}
edit_file

Performs exact string replacements in files. Usage: - You must read the file before editing; this tool errors otherwise. - Preserve the exact source indentation from the read output, and never include the read status header in old_string or new_string. - Prefer editing an existing file over creating a new one. - Only use emojis if the user explicitly requests it.

{
  "properties": {
    "file_path": {
      "description": "Absolute path to the file to edit. Must be absolute, not relative.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "new_string": {
      "description": "The text to replace old_string with. Must be different from old_string.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "old_string": {
      "description": "The exact text to find and replace. Must be unique in the file unless replace_all is True.",
      "type": "string"
    },
    "replace_all": {
      "default": false,
      "description": "If True, replace all occurrences of old_string. If False (default), old_string must be unique.",
      "type": "boolean"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "file_path",
    "old_string",
    "new_string"
  ],
  "type": "object"
}

# Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: complete
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: command `python -I -B -c <unittest runner>`; suite `test_slug.py`; status `not_yet_written`; tests_run 0; exit_code null; stderr `test_slug.py does not exist.` This was acceptable because tests are created by SLUG-002.
  - Independent review: approved. Reviewer confirmed `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify(value)`, includes the required plain-language header, uses only standard-library `re` and `unicodedata`, satisfies representative examples and separator collapse, and has no network/filesystem/subprocess/service dependencies or actionable findings.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: pending
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: not yet run for this task.
  - Independent review: not yet reviewed.

read_file

Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. # Plan: small, well-tested Python slug function

## Scope and contract
- Public API: `slugify` imported from `/slug.py` as `from slug import slugify`.
- Implementation must be inspectable, deterministic, standard-library only, and independent of external services.
- Test execution is performed automatically by the workflow by running `/test_slug.py` after each worker invocation; only workflow-supplied execution and independent reviewer evidence may be recorded as verification.

## Tasks

### SLUG-001 — Implement `slugify` in `/slug.py`
- Status: complete
- Writable files for worker: `/slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Provide public function `slugify(value)`.
  - Accept text input and return a lowercase ASCII slug string.
  - Normalize Unicode characters to reasonable ASCII equivalents where possible using the Python standard library.
  - Replace runs of non-alphanumeric characters with a single hyphen.
  - Strip leading and trailing hyphens.
  - Return an empty string when no alphanumeric content remains.
  - Be deterministic and independent of services or process/global mutable state.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
  - Keep implementation inspectable and small; add comments only for non-obvious choices.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify`.
  - Representative expected examples include: `slugify("Hello, World!") == "hello-world"`, `slugify(" Café déjà vu ") == "cafe-deja-vu"`, `slugify("---") == ""`, and whitespace/punctuation runs collapse to one hyphen.
  - Uses only standard-library code and no network, filesystem, subprocess, or service dependencies.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.
- Evidence:
  - Test execution: command `python -I -B -c <unittest runner>`; suite `test_slug.py`; status `not_yet_written`; tests_run 0; exit_code null; stderr `test_slug.py does not exist.` This was acceptable because tests are created by SLUG-002.
  - Independent review: approved. Reviewer confirmed `/slug.py` defines importable `slugify(value)`, includes the required plain-language header, uses only standard-library `re` and `unicodedata`, satisfies representative examples and separator collapse, and has no network/filesystem/subprocess/service dependencies or actionable findings.

### SLUG-002 — Write behavior tests in `/test_slug.py`
- Status: complete
- Writable files for worker: `/test_slug.py`
- Required behavior:
  - Create standard-library `unittest` tests importing `slugify` from `slug`.
  - Cover ordinary phrase slugging, repeated separator collapse, trimming of separator boundaries, Unicode normalization to ASCII, empty/no-content results, and preservation of digits/letters in lowercase form.
  - Tests should verify public behavior rather than implementation internals.
  - Include a plain-language file header documenting purpose, responsibilities, assumptions, AI assistance, and copyright status.
- Acceptance criteria:
  - `/test_slug.py` is directly executable with standard-library unittest conventions.
  - Tests distinguish plausible incorrect implementations such as leaving punctuation, failing Unicode normalization, failing lowercase conversion, or emitting duplicate/edge hyphens.
  - Automatic `/test_slug.py` execution evidence and independent review outcome will be recorded after worker completion.

  - Independent review: approved. """Small slug creation helper.

Purpose: provide a single public function, ``slugify``, for turning text into
lowercase ASCII slugs suitable for simple identifiers or URL path segments.
Responsibilities: normalize Unicode text with the Python standard library,
remove characters that cannot be represented as ASCII, collapse every run of
non-alphanumeric characters to one hyphen, and trim hyphens at the edges.
Assumptions: callers pass text input; non-ASCII characters without a standard
ASCII decomposition are omitted rather than transliterated by an external
service or dependency.
AI assistance: this file was drafted with AI assistance under human direction.
Copyright status: no copyright claim is asserted for this exercise artifact.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

_SEPARATOR_RUN = re.compile(r"[^a-z0-9]+")


def slugify(value: str) -> str:
    """Return a deterministic lowercase ASCII slug for text input."""
    normalized = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", value)
    ascii_text = normalized.encode("ascii", "ignore").decode("ascii")
    lowercase_text = ascii_text.lower()
    return _SEPARATOR_RUN.sub("-", lowercase_text).strip("-")
Status: ok

"""Behavior tests for the slug helper.

Purpose: verify the public ``slugify`` function turns text into predictable
lowercase ASCII slugs.
Responsibilities: exercise externally visible behavior for punctuation removal,
separator collapse, edge trimming, Unicode normalization, empty results, and
letter/digit preservation.
Assumptions: the implementation is imported as ``slugify`` from the local
``slug`` module and is tested only through that public function.
AI assistance: this test file was drafted with AI assistance under human
direction.
Copyright status: no copyright claim is asserted for this exercise artifact.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import unittest

from slug import slugify


class SlugifyBehaviorTests(unittest.TestCase):
    """Public behavior tests for slugify."""

    def test_slugifies_ordinary_phrase_and_removes_punctuation(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify("Hello, World!"), "hello-world")

    def test_collapses_repeated_separators_to_single_hyphen(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify("alpha   beta---gamma___delta"), "alpha-beta-gamma-delta")

    def test_trims_separator_boundaries(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify(" -- Keep edges clean!! "), "keep-edges-clean")

    def test_normalizes_unicode_to_ascii_when_possible(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify(" Café déjà vu "), "cafe-deja-vu")

    def test_returns_empty_string_when_no_alphanumeric_content_remains(self) -> None:
        examples = ["", "---", " ", "!!!", "東京"]
        for text in examples:
            with self.subTest(text=text):
                self.assertEqual(slugify(text), "")

    def test_preserves_digits_and_letters_in_lowercase_form(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify("Release V2 Build 123ABC"), "release-v2-build-123abc")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
Status: ok

"""Small slug creation helper.

Purpose: provide a single public function, ``slugify``, for turning text into
lowercase ASCII slugs suitable for simple identifiers or URL path segments.
Responsibilities: normalize Unicode text with the Python standard library,
remove characters that cannot be represented as ASCII, collapse every run of
non-alphanumeric characters to one hyphen, and trim hyphens at the edges.
Assumptions: callers pass text input; non-ASCII characters without a standard
ASCII decomposition are omitted rather than transliterated by an external
service or dependency.
AI assistance: this file was drafted with AI assistance under human direction.
Copyright status: no copyright claim is asserted for this exercise artifact.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

_SEPARATOR_RUN = re.compile(r"[^a-z0-9]+")


def slugify(value: str) -> str:
    """Return a deterministic lowercase ASCII slug for text input."""
    normalized = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", value)
    ascii_text = normalized.encode("ascii", "ignore").decode("ascii")
    lowercase_text = ascii_text.lower()
    return _SEPARATOR_RUN.sub("-", lowercase_text).strip("-")
Tool result · tokens unreported
@@ lines 1-48 of 48 @@
"""Behavior tests for the slug helper.

Purpose: verify the public ``slugify`` function turns text into predictable
lowercase ASCII slugs.
Responsibilities: exercise externally visible behavior for punctuation removal,
separator collapse, edge trimming, Unicode normalization, empty results, and
letter/digit preservation.
Assumptions: the implementation is imported as ``slugify`` from the local
``slug`` module and is tested only through that public function.
AI assistance: this test file was drafted with AI assistance under human
direction.
Copyright status: no copyright claim is asserted for this exercise artifact.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import unittest

from slug import slugify


class SlugifyBehaviorTests(unittest.TestCase):
    """Public behavior tests for slugify."""

    def test_slugifies_ordinary_phrase_and_removes_punctuation(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify("Hello, World!"), "hello-world")

    def test_collapses_repeated_separators_to_single_hyphen(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify("alpha   beta---gamma___delta"), "alpha-beta-gamma-delta")

    def test_trims_separator_boundaries(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify(" -- Keep edges clean!! "), "keep-edges-clean")

    def test_normalizes_unicode_to_ascii_when_possible(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify(" Café déjà vu "), "cafe-deja-vu")

    def test_returns_empty_string_when_no_alphanumeric_content_remains(self) -> None:
        examples = ["", "---", " ", "!!!", "東京"]
        for text in examples:
            with self.subTest(text=text):
                self.assertEqual(slugify(text), "")

    def test_preserves_digits_and_letters_in_lowercase_form(self) -> None:
        self.assertEqual(slugify("Release V2 Build 123ABC"), "release-v2-build-123abc")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

read_file
01a1168f-5396-7c30-813d-51767827827bReads a file from the filesystem. Assume any path the user provides is valid; reading a missing file returns an error. Usage: - By default, it reads up to 100 lines starting from the beginning of the file. Use `offset`/`limit` to page through large files instead of reading them whole. - A status header, `@@ field | field | ... @@`, sits above the file content, and every line after it is verbatim file content. When content is truncated, there may be an explanation before the header. Never include the header when editing. - Speculatively batch multiple `read_file` calls in one response when several files may be useful. - An empty file returns a system-reminder warning in place of contents. - Large tool results may be offloaded to a file; the tool message gives the path. Read that path here, paging with `offset`/`limit`. - Images (`.png`, `.jpg`, etc.), audio, video, and PDFs return multimodal content blocks (https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langchain/messages#multimodal). - For images and PDFs, pagination via `offset`/`limit` is text-only - supply `file_path` only - Always read a file before editing it.
